 - The vulnerability has received a maximum severity score of 10 on the CVSS scale and is currently being exploited.

Google and Microsoft have addressed the vulnerabilities associated with this attack, which highlights potential risks posed by AI integration in browsers.

 - The attack exploits permissions granted to AI components, enabling access to sensitive user data.
 - Major browser vendors like Google and Microsoft have recognized and fixed the associated vulnerabilities.

In 2025, California completed a significant pipeline project that involved burying a gas pipeline and removing a concrete barrier that had blocked migratory fish for decades. As a result, Chinook salmon accessed previously unreachable areas of the watershed for the first time since the 1950s, marking a notable step in the ongoing environmental restoration efforts in the region. This project reconnected over 20 miles of upstream habitat for various migratory fish species, including steelhead and salmon, and is part of a decades-long initiative to enhance the ecosystem of Alameda Creek.

 - California buried a gas pipeline and removed a barrier that blocked migratory fish.
 - Chinook salmon returned to an upstream watershed for the first time since the 1950s.
 - The project is an essential part of a long-term restoration effort for over 20 miles of fish habitat.

Source: https://timesofindia.indiatimes.com/science/nature/in-2025-california-buried-a-gas-pipeline-20-feet-below-alameda-creek-and-removed-its-final-8-foot-barrier-that-same-week-chinook-salmon-reached-this-part-of-the-watershed-for-the-first-time-since-the-1950s/articleshow/134350679.cms

Category: Other
Importance: 8
In 2025, a significant restoration project in California removed the last major barrier to fish migration on Alameda Creek, allowing Chinook salmon to access upstream habitat for the first time since the 1950s. This project involved relocating a gas pipeline and reshaping the creek, facilitating the reopening of over 20 miles of spawning and juvenile-rearing habitats for fish species. The restoration reflects a nearly three-decade effort to reconnect a fragmented watershed, benefiting endangered species such as the Central California Coast steelhead.

 - California's removal of a major obstacle to fish migration has allowed Chinook salmon to return to previously inaccessible habitats.
 - The restoration efforts span nearly 30 years, aiming to enhance the ecosystem for migratory fish in the region.

India has mandated that caller-ID and call-management apps share users' spam reports with telecom operators, aiming to improve spam call tracking and enforcement. Truecaller, a popular spam-blocking app, argues this requirement is anti-competitive and raises concerns about the transfer of valuable data to telecom companies. Additionally, new regulations will require companies using AI for calls to disclose their use to telecom operators and will classify certain automated calls as spam if not declared in advance.

 - India's telecom regulator (TRAI) requires caller-ID applications to share spam reports with telecom operators for better enforcement against spam calls.
 - Truecaller claims that the regulation is anti-competitive, transferring valuable data from apps to telecom companies.
 - New rules classify automated calls using AI under a specific regulatory framework, mandating disclosure by companies making such calls.

Source: https://techcrunch.com/?p=3166409
